in short, the runner:\\n+\\n+- builds `sawc2` and every unit program (`compiler/<stage>/tests/*.saw`) with the\\n+  frozen compiler, and runs each unit program: exit 0 is a pass;\\n+- compares `sawc2 lex` with each golden fixture in `compiler/tests/lex/`\\n+  (`NAME.saw` beside its expected `NAME.tokens`, and `NAME.docs` for doc\\n+  trivia), byte for byte;\\n+- refuses a fixture line that ends in whitespace, or a fixture that ends in a\\n+  blank line, naming the file and line: the patch server applies patches with\\n+  `git apply --whitespace=fix`, which strips both;\\n+- fails if a token kind the lexer declares, or a kind of lex error it reports,\\n+  appears in no fixture;\\n+- runs the subset checker (`compiler/tools/subset_check.py`) over the compiler's\\n+  own source, and over its fixtures in `compiler/tests/subset/`, whose\\n+  `// refuses: RULE` markers name exactly what each must report.\\n+\\n+It prints one line per failure, then one summary line, and exits 1 on any\\n+failure. The golden fixtures replaced the lexer differential (`lexdiff`): they\\n+were checked once against the Python lexer, and they are the lexer's oracle now.\\n+\\n+### The per-patch gate\\n+\\n+`./build.sh test` is what sawtracker runs on every submitted patch, as\\n+`./build.sh test --changed-since HEAD^` (`.sawtracker/tests.json`). The\\n+decision is `tools/patch_gate.py`'s:\\n+\\n+- the compiler tests above always run;\\n+- the Python suite runs when a changed path is one of `test_runner.py`'s inputs\\n+  (`sawc/`, `examples/`, the vector files under `tests/` the examples read, the\\n+  runner itself);\\n+- the freestanding suite runs when a changed path is one of its runner's inputs\\n+  (`sawc/`, `tests/freestanding/`, `blade/`, `libs/`, the runner itself);\\n+- a change to the gate itself (`build.sh`, `tools/patch_gate.py`,\\n+  `.sawtracker/`) runs everything, and so does a run whose changed paths are\\n+  unknown.\\n+\\n+It prints each part's decision with its reason. `--dry-run` prints the decision\\n+and runs nothing, for a revision (`--changed-since REV`) or a patch file\\n+(`--diff FILE`), which is how to see what a squashed patch will cost before\\n+submitting it:\\n+\\n+```bash\\n+./build.sh test --dry-run --diff /tmp/patch.diff\\n+```\\n+\\n+`build.sh` holds the suite lock around everything the gate runs. From a\\n+worktree, point `SAW_PYTHON` at the main checkout's venv rather than letting it\\n+bootstrap one.\\n+\\n ## The Gate Battery\\n \\n ```bash\\n@@ -548,17 +605,13 @@ a stand-in `$PATH`, a pre-populated fetch cache — so no step depends on what\\n happens to be installed on the machine, and the fetch step is exercised without\\n touching the network.\\n \\n-### The selfhostlex stage\\n+### The compiler stage\\n \\n-The Aug-10 coverage sweep mapped which battery stage semantically checks\\n-each test tree: `blade/tests` and `libs/*/tests` run inside `bootstrap`\\n-(and ONLY there — `--quick` skips them; `sos/tests` gated inside the\\n-`sos` stage until both left for the sawos repo at 238 unit 5), and\\n-`selfhost/lexer/tests` ran NOWHERE — nine passing tests no stage\\n-compiled. `selfhostlex` closes that: each `selfhost/lexer/tests/*.saw`\\n-is compiled and run, exit 0 = pass, same contract as `blade test`.\\n-(`tests/freestanding` joined that map with the `freestanding` stage above,\\n-and runs there only.)\\n+Runs `compiler/tests/run.py`, the self-hosted compiler's tests (above). It is\\n+where the compiler's unit programs are compiled and run; no other stage opens\\n+them. For the other test trees: `blade/tests` and `libs/*/tests` run inside\\n+`bootstrap` only (so `--quick` skips them), and `tests/freestanding` runs inside\\n+the `freestanding` stage only.\\n \\n ### The forgetgate stage\\n \\n@@ -1312,7 +1365,7 @@ Put the token where the client can find it (`~/.config/saw-worker/token`, or\\n # itself is Saw (devtools/irdet/); this driver builds it and splits the corpus.\\n ./.venv/bin/python tools/irdet_remote.py --all --remote studio.local:8710\\n \\n-# The whole battery on the worker: suite, lexdiff, astdiff, irdet --all\\n+# The whole battery on the worker: suite, compiler, astdiff, irdet --all\\n ./.venv/bin/python tools/remote_battery.py --remote studio.local:8710\\n ```\\n \\ndiff --git a/build.sh b/build.sh\\nindex 5d2607ee..fe3d0b5b 100755\\n--- a/build.sh\\n+++ b/build.sh\\n@@ -1,23 +1,44 @@\\n #!/bin/sh\\n # The sawtracker patch gate's entry point: `.sawtracker/tests.json` runs\\n-# `./build.sh test` on every submitted patch, in the server's own clone.\\n-# Also fine to run by hand. Bootstraps .venv when absent, then runs the\\n-# compiler's per-commit gate — full suite + the freestanding feature\\n-# suite, both arches — under the machine-wide suite lock (TESTING.md)\\n-# so a server-side patch test never overlaps a local suite run.\\n+# `./build.sh test --changed-since HEAD^` on every submitted patch, in the\\n+# server's own clone, where HEAD is the applied patch and HEAD^ its base. Also\\n+# fine to run by hand:\\n+#\\n+#   ./build.sh test                        everything\\n+#   ./build.sh test --changed-since REV    what the change since REV needs\\n+#   ./build.sh test --diff FILE            what a patch file needs\\n+#   ./build.sh test ... --dry-run          print that decision, run nothing\\n+#\\n+# tools/patch_gate.py decides which suites a change needs and runs them. This\\n+# script bootstraps .venv when absent (SAW_PYTHON names an interpreter to use\\n+# instead, as from a worktree) and holds the machine-wide suite lock around the\\n+# run (TESTING.md), so a server-side patch test never overlaps a local suite.\\n set -e\\n \\n cd \\\"$(dirname \\\"$0\\\")\\\"\\n \\n case \\\"${1:-test}\\\" in\\n-  test) ;;\\n-  *) echo \\\"usage: ./build.sh test\\\" >&2; exit 2 ;;\\n+  test) if [ $# -gt 0 ]; then shift; fi ;;\\n+  *) echo \\\"usage: ./build.sh test [--changed-since REV | --diff FILE] [--dry-run]\\\" >&2\\n+     exit 2 ;;\\n esac\\n \\n-if [ ! -x .venv/bin/python ]; then\\n-    python3 -m venv .venv\\n-    .venv/bin/python -m pip install --quiet --upgrade pip\\n-    .venv/bin/python -m pip install --quiet -r sawc/requirements.txt\\n+# A dry run reads git and nothing else, so it needs neither the venv nor the lock.\\n+for arg in \\\"$@\\\"; do\\n+    if [ \\\"$arg\\\" = \\\"--dry-run\\\" ]; then\\n+        exec \\\"${SAW_PYTHON:-python3}\\\" tools/patch_gate.py \\\"$@\\\"\\n+    fi\\n+done\\n+\\n+if [ -n \\\"${SAW_PYTHON:-}\\\" ]; then\\n+    PY=\\\"$SAW_PYTHON\\\"\\n+else\\n+    if [ ! -x .venv/bin/python ]; then\\n+        python3 -m venv .venv\\n+        .venv/bin/python -m pip install --quiet --upgrade pip\\n+        .venv/bin/python -m pip install --quiet -r sawc/requirements.txt\\n+    fi\\n+    PY=.venv/bin/python\\n fi\\n \\n LOCK=\\\"/private/tmp/claude-$(id -u)/saw-suite-lock\\\"\\n@@ -25,5 +46,4 @@ mkdir -p \\\"$(dirname \\\"$LOCK\\\")\\\"\\n until mkdir \\\"$LOCK\\\" 2>/dev/null; do sleep 15; done\\n trap 'rmdir \\\"$LOCK\\\"' EXIT INT TERM\\n \\n-.venv/bin/python test_runner.py\\n-.venv/bin/python tools/freestanding_runner.py\\n+\\\"$PY\\\" tools/patch_gate.py \\\"$@\\\"\\ndiff --git a/compiler/README.md b/compiler/README.md\\nnew file mode 100644\\nindex 00000000..928e1ec9\\n--- /dev/null\\n+++ b/compiler/README.md\\n@@ -0,0 +1,199 @@\\n+# compiler/: the self-hosted Saw compiler\\n+\\n+The Saw compiler written in Saw (epic SL-398). Until it builds itself, the\\n+frozen Python compiler in `sawc/` builds it: that is bootstrap Stage 0. The\\n+design lives in the tracker's docs, which `~/bin/sawtracker doc show NAME`\\n+prints:\\n+\\n+- **SL:architecture**: the stages and their contracts (§3), the shared\\n+  representation (§3.0), and the bootstrap and the subset (§4).\\n+- **SL:hazards**: the shapes Stage 0 mishandles, and which of them the subset\\n+  checker refuses.\\n+- **SL:testing** and **SL:borrowing**: the locked-down language the compiler\\n+  implements.\\n+\\n+## Layout\\n+\\n+```\\n+compiler/\\n+  lex/                the lexer, package `sawlex` (lex/README.md has its dump format)\\n+    src/lib.saw\\n+    tests/*.saw       unit programs\\n+  driver/             the `sawc2` binary, package `sawc2`\\n+    src/main.saw\\n+  tools/\\n+    build.py          builds sawc2, or any program over the stage packages\\n+    subset_check.py   the subset checker\\n+  tests/\\n+    run.py            the test runner\\n+    lex/              golden token fixtures\\n+    subset/           the subset checker's own fixtures\\n+```\\n+\\n+Each stage has its own directory and is a package, with its source under\\n+`src/`. The others import it as `<package>.src.<module>`, through\\n+`--module-path`. `STAGE_PACKAGES` in `tools/build.py` is the one table of those\\n+packages, and every build goes through `build_program` there. The driver is\\n+named `sawc2` for now.\\n+\\n+## Building\\n+\\n+Use the venv's Python, since it runs the frozen compiler:\\n+\\n+```sh\\n+./.venv/bin/python compiler/tools/build.py            # builds .build/sawc2\\n+.build/sawc2 lex [--docs] <file.saw>                   # the token dump, or the doc trivia\\n+.build/sawc2 lex --kinds                               # every token kind's dump name\\n+./.venv/bin/python compiler/tools/build.py ENTRY.saw -o OUT   # another program\\n+```\\n+\\n+A lex error prints one `ERROR` record and exits 1. A usage or I/O failure exits 2.\\n+\\n+## Testing\\n+\\n+```sh\\n+./.venv/bin/python compiler/tests/run.py\\n+```\\n+\\n+The runner prints each failure on its own line, then one summary line, and exits\\n+1 if anything failed. Its output is deterministic. The battery's `compiler` lane\\n+runs it, and so does every per-patch gate run (`./build.sh test`). It checks four\\n+things.\\n+\\n+- **Unit programs**: `compiler/<stage>/tests/*.saw`. These are small programs in\\n+  the subset, built by the frozen compiler against the stage packages. Each\\n+  passes by exiting 0. Until the compiler builds itself, these and the golden\\n+  dumps are its only tests. At self-hosting, `*.test.saw` sidecars take over\\n+  (SL:testing §4).\\n+- **Golden token fixtures**: `tests/lex/NAME.saw`, with the expected\\n+  `sawc2 lex` output in `NAME.tokens`. Where doc trivia matter, `NAME.docs` holds\\n+  the expected `sawc2 lex --docs` output. Each fixture's first line names its\\n+  source, which is a tracked `.saw` file or the inputs of a lexer unit program.\\n+  The fixtures are the lexer's oracle. When the lexer changes on purpose,\\n+  regenerate the affected expectations with `sawc2 lex` and review the diff.\\n+  No fixture line may end in whitespace, and no fixture may end in a blank line:\\n+  the patch server's `git apply --whitespace=fix` and most editors strip both,\\n+  so the runner refuses them by file and line.\\n+- **Coverage**: every token kind appears in some `.tokens` file. The kinds come\\n+  from the lexer itself: `sawc2 lex --kinds` prints `token_kinds()`, and the\\n+  runner checks its length against the `TokenKind` cases as declared. Every kind\\n+  of lex error appears too, taken from the messages of the lexer's own\\n+  `err`/`err_at` calls.\\n+- **The subset checker**, over the compiler's source and over its own fixtures\\n+  (below).\\n+\\n+## The subset\\n+\\n+The compiler's own source is written in the subset of SL:architecture §4. It is\\n+the intersection of today's Saw and the locked-down language, with the shapes\\n+Stage 0 mishandles taken out. `tools/subset_check.py` enforces it over every\\n+`compiler/**/*.saw` except `compiler/tests/**` (fixtures hold arbitrary Saw) and\\n+`*.test.saw` (sidecars, which only Stage 1 builds). It reads the source through\\n+the frozen compiler's own lexer and parser, so it checks exactly what Stage 0\\n+reads.\\n+\\n+```sh\\n+./.venv/bin/python compiler/tools/subset_check.py              # the tree\\n+./.venv/bin/python compiler/tools/subset_check.py FILE...      # these files\\n+```\\n+\\n+A diagnostic reads `file:line: rule: message (source)`. The driver and the stage\\n+packages form one build, and each unit program is a build of its own over the\\n+stage packages. That matters for the rules that compare declarations across a\\n+build. The rules read every region of a file: inline `module m { ... }` bodies\\n+(`inline-module` refuses the module itself), trait default bodies, `init`\\n+bodies, `static` initializers, `static_assert` conditions, and the text of each\\n+interpolation.\\n+\\n+Each rule has at least one fixture, `tests/subset/RULE.saw` or\\n+`tests/subset/RULE.VARIANT.saw`, whose `// refuses: RULE...` markers name\\n+exactly the lines and rules the checker must report; a rule named twice on a line\\n+expects two findings of it there, each with its own message. The `clean`\\n+fixtures, which hold the spellings SL:hazards recommends and ordinary subset\\n+code, must report nothing. The runner fails for a rule with no fixture.\\n+\\n+| Rule | Refuses | Source |\\n+|---|---|---|\\n+| `lex`, `parse` | a file the frozen lexer or parser rejects | §4 |\\n+| `file-end` | a file that does not end in a newline | C2 |\\n+| `sync-only` | tasks, threads, channels, `sleep`, a `blocking` extern | §4 |\\n+| `closure-capture` | a closure naming an enclosing binding or `self`, a capture list | §4 |\\n+| `type-alias` | any `type` alias | §4, S9 |\\n+| `prelude-type-name` | a declared type named like a public std type, import-gated ones included | §4, L17 |\\n+| `selective-imports` | `import m` and `import m.*` | §4, L3 |\\n+| `import-allowlist` | a std module off `ALLOWED_STD_MODULES`, or a package that is not a compiler stage | §4 |\\n+| `std-api` | a std call off `STD_API`; `m[k]` on a Map; a write, a mutating method or a presence test through `get`; `m[k]! = v`; `with_ref`, `with_var_ref`, `with_unique`, a closure-taking `lock` | §4 |\\n+| `borrows-accessor` | `borrows`, `lend`, `lends`, and a `borrows struct` | §4 |\\n+| `generic-extension-init` | an `init` in an extension with type parameters | §4, S1 |\\n+| `integer-overload` | an overload set where a member takes a non-`Int` integer | §4, L1 |\\n+| `any-type` | `any` in a type | §4, S12 |\\n+| `box-type` | `Box` | §4, L8 |\\n+| `cell-type` | `Mutex`, `SpinLock`, `Once`, `UnsafeMutableInterior`, `Atomic`, `Arc` | §4, S11, S15 |\\n+| `raw-pointer` | pointer types, std's `unsafe struct`s, `unsafe`, `*p`, `move *p` | §4, C1 |\\n+| `fixed-array` | `[T; N]` types, repeat literals, and an array literal with no written type to adopt | §4, L7 |\\n+| `value-loop` | `break` with an operand | §4, L11 |\\n+| `statement-arm` | a match arm whose body is a bare statement | §4, L13, C4 |\\n+| `borrow-syntax` | `borrow` as a name | §4 |\\n+| `test-directive` | `@test` | §4 |\\n+| `deinit-body` | a `deinit` in an extension, a conformance or a trait body | leak tolerance |\\n+| `borrowed-match-payload` | `move` of an arm binding, or a `match` on one, when the scrutinee is borrowed | S2 |\\n+| `root-reuse` | an argument reading the receiver's or a `&` argument's root; an index write whose right side reads its root | S4 |\\n+| `var-ref-into-let` | `&var`, or a call of a `&var self` method, reaching into a binding that is not a `var`, or into any `static` | S5 |\\n+| `function-exit` | a value-returning body, `init` or closure that can fall off its end or end in a `Void` call; code after `return`, `break` or `continue` | S6 |\\n+| `int-literal-range` | an unsuffixed integer literal above `Int.max`, except as a `UInt64` binding's value or as `-9223372036854775808` | S7 |\\n+| `enum-equatable-body` | a hand-written `equals` on an enum, in its `Equatable` conformance or apart from it | S8 |\\n+| `nested-optional` | `T??`, and an optional element or value type in `Vector`, `Map` or `Set` | S10 |\\n+| `argument-labels` | a repeated label; a labeled call to a `borrows` accessor, std's or the build's, judged by the receiver's type | S14 |\\n+| `index-receiver-call` | a method not known to only read, on storage reached through `x[i]`, `m[k]?.` or a tuple element | S15 |\\n+| `cast-then-optional` | `?` or `??` after an `as` cast's type, on its line or the next | S17 |\\n+| `program-unique-names` | a free function or `static` named like another in the build or in std; a type named like another in the build, since the checker keys its facts about types by name | S18 |\\n+| `written-type-shape` | a written type nested 8 levels, `Optional<T>`, `Self` inside a tuple type | §4, S19 |\\n+| `float-literal` | any float literal | §4, S20 |\\n+| `interpolation-line-break` | a line break inside an interpolation's braces | S21 |\\n+| `inline-module` | every inline `module name { }` | S22 |\\n+| `from-raw-literal` | `from(raw:)` with a bare literal | L1 |\\n+| `closure-syntax` | an unannotated closure parameter, `$0`, a trailing closure, a function type under `?` | L2 |\\n+| `default-value-literal` | a default parameter value that is not a literal | L3 |\\n+| `optional-shaping` | `== None`, a method directly on a `get` result, a collection literal returned as a `Result` | L4 |\\n+| `empty-struct` | a struct with no fields | L5 |\\n+| `bounded-extension` | a bound on an extension's type parameter | L6 |\\n+| `name-collision` | a generic method and generic function sharing a name (std included), a static and an instance method sharing one, a type parameter spelled like a type | L9 |\\n+| `type-param-receiver` | a call whose receiver is a type parameter | L10 |\\n+| `leading-minus` | a line that begins with `-` after a token that can end an operand | L12 |\\n+| `nesting-depth`, `chain-length` | brackets nested past 30; an operator, `??`, postfix, `else if` or `else if let` chain past 100 | L14 |\\n+| `generic-extension-params` | an extension head naming a generic type, the build's or std's, without its type parameters, as `extension Gen { }`, `extension Gen: NoCopy {}` or `extension Vector { }` | L18 |\\n+| `interpolation-content` | `//`, a brace or a quote inside an interpolation | C3 |\\n+\\n+§4 is SL:architecture §4; the other codes are SL:hazards entries, and \\\"leak\\n+tolerance\\\" is its introduction's \\\"Leaks are tolerated in Stage 1\\\". A hazard that\\n+only leaks has no rule: Stage 1 is replaced after it compiles the source, so a\\n+leak costs it memory, never output. That holds while no skipped drop has an\\n+effect, which is why the source declares no `deinit` and why\\n+`ALLOWED_STD_MODULES` is pinned in `tests/run.py`: adding a std module means\\n+checking that every `deinit` it reaches only frees memory or closes a\\n+descriptor. S13, a nested generic with defaulted parameters, only leaks, so it\\n+has no rule.\\n+\\n+Several checks are syntactic, so they are partial. What they cannot see stays a\\n+trust obligation, as SL:hazards describes:\\n+\\n+- `std-api` traces a receiver's type through written types: bindings, fields,\\n+  the build's function and method returns (untraced when overloads return\\n+  different types, since which overload a call picks is not traced), `get` and\\n+  subscripts, `self` in an extension or a trait default body, a match-arm\\n+  binding (from the scrutinee's enum, `Optional` or `Result` payload), and a\\n+  destructured tuple's elements. A\\n+  method on a traced receiver is the owning type's. A type parameter's methods\\n+  are its bounds': one a build trait declares is the build's. On an untraced\\n+  receiver, a name std declares counts as std's, even when a compiler type\\n+  declares it too, so an instance method on the allowlist is allowed on every\\n+  untraced receiver. `m[k]` is caught when the receiver traces to `Map`, or when\\n+  the subscript is used as an optional.\\n+- `closure-capture`, `root-reuse` and `var-ref-into-let` resolve names through\\n+  lexical scopes. They do not follow values. A path headed by an inline module's\\n+  name, such as `m.inner.NAME`, is followed through the module bodies it names:\\n+  `var-ref-into-let` finds the `static` behind `&var m.NAME`, and `std-api`\\n+  knows a call through `m.inner.f(...)` is the build's own.\\n+- `nested-optional` sees written types only, not the types a generic\\n+  instantiation produces.\\n+- SL:hazards S16 and L15 have no check.\\ndiff --git a/compiler/driver/Saw.toml b/compiler/driver/Saw.toml\\nnew file mode 100644\\nindex 00000000..be7e9fc8\\n--- /dev/null\\n+++ b/compiler/driver/Saw.toml\\n@@ -0,0 +1,6 @@\\n+[package]\\n+name = \\\"sawc2\\\"\\n+version = \\\"0.1.0\\\"\\n+\\n+[dependencies]\\n+sawlex = { path = \\\"../lex\\\" }\\ndiff --git a/compiler/driver/src/main.saw b/compiler/driver/src/main.saw\\nnew file mode 100644\\nindex 00000000..f89c04e2\\n--- /dev/null\\n+++ b/compiler/driver/src/main.saw\\n@@ -0,0 +1,146 @@\\n+// sawc2, the driver of the self-hosted Saw compiler. Each stage is reached\\n+// through a subcommand; the lexer is the first.\\n+//\\n+//   sawc2 lex [--docs] <file.saw>   the token dump, or the doc-comment trivia\\n+//   sawc2 lex --kinds               every token kind's dump name, one per line\\n+//\\n+// The record formats are the lexer's canonical dump (compiler/lex/README.md).\\n+// A lex error prints one ERROR record and exits 1; a usage or I/O failure\\n+// prints a message and exits 2.\\n+\\n+import sawlex.src.lib.{LexResult, format_doc, format_error, format_token, kind_name, lex, token_kinds}\\n+import std.env.{Env}\\n+import std.file.{File}\\n+import std.path.{Path}\\n+\\n+static DRIVER_EXIT_LEX_ERROR: Int = 1\\n+static DRIVER_EXIT_USAGE: Int = 2\\n+\\n+func driver_usage() -> Int {\\n+    print(\\\"usage: sawc2 lex [--docs] <file.saw>\\\")\\n+    print(\\\"       sawc2 lex --kinds\\\")\\n+    DRIVER_EXIT_USAGE\\n+}\\n+\\n+func main() -> Int {\\n+    let args = try! Env.args()\\n+    let command = args.get(1) ?? \\\"\\\"\\n+    if command.equals(\\\"lex\\\") {\\n+        return driver_lex(&args)\\n+    }\\n+    driver_usage()\\n+}\\n+\\n+// `sawc2 lex`: the arguments after the subcommand name one file, or ask for\\n+// the kind list.\\n+func driver_lex(args: &Vector<String>) -> Int {\\n+    var docs_mode = false\\n+    var kinds_mode = false\\n+    var path = \\\"\\\"\\n+    var i = 2\\n+    while i < args.len() {\\n+        let arg = args.get(i) ?? \\\"\\\"\\n+        if arg.equals(\\\"--docs\\\") {\\n+            docs_mode = true\\n+        } else if arg.equals(\\\"--kinds\\\") {\\n+            kinds_mode = true\\n+        } else if arg.starts_with(\\\"--\\\") || not path.equals(\\\"\\\") {\\n+            return driver_usage()\\n+        } else {\\n+            path = arg\\n+        }\\n+        i = i + 1\\n+    }\\n+    if kinds_mode {\\n+        if docs_mode || not path.equals(\\\"\\\") {\\n+            return driver_usage()\\n+        }\\n+        return driver_print_kinds()\\n+    }\\n+    if path.equals(\\\"\\\") {\\n+        return driver_usage()\\n+    }\\n+    if let text = driver_read_source(path) {\\n+        driver_dump(text, docs_mode)\\n+    } else {\\n+        DRIVER_EXIT_USAGE\\n+    }\\n+}\\n+\\n+// The file's text, or None once the reason it could not be read is printed.\\n+func driver_read_source(path: String) -> String? {\\n+    let opened = File.open(Path(s: path))\\n+    var file = match opened {\\n+        case Ok(handle) -> move handle,\\n+        case Err(e) -> {\\n+            print(\\\"sawc2: cannot open {path}: {e}\\\")\\n+            return None\\n+        }\\n+    }\\n+    let read = file.read()\\n+    let data = match read {\\n+        case Ok(bytes) -> move bytes,\\n+        case Err(e) -> {\\n+            print(\\\"sawc2: cannot read {path}: {e}\\\")\\n+            return None\\n+        }\\n+    }\\n+    let decoded = data.to_string()\\n+    match decoded {\\n+        case Ok(text) -> text,\\n+        case Err(e) -> {\\n+            print(\\\"sawc2: {path} is not valid UTF-8 (byte {e.offset})\\\")\\n+            None\\n+        }\\n+    }\\n+}\\n+\\n+func driver_dump(source: String, docs_mode: Bool) -> Int {\\n+    let lexed = lex(source)\\n+    match lexed {\\n+        case Ok(result) -> {\\n+            if docs_mode {\\n+                driver_print_docs(&result)\\n+            } else {\\n+                driver_print_tokens(&result)\\n+            }\\n+            0\\n+        },\\n+        case Err(e) -> {\\n+            print(format_error(&e))\\n+            DRIVER_EXIT_LEX_ERROR\\n+        }\\n+    }\\n+}\\n+\\n+func driver_print_tokens(result: &LexResult) {\\n+    var i = 0\\n+    while i < result.tokens.len() {\\n+        if let t = result.tokens.get(i) {\\n+            print(format_token(&t, &result.segments))\\n+        }\\n+        i = i + 1\\n+    }\\n+}\\n+\\n+func driver_print_docs(result: &LexResult) {\\n+    var i = 0\\n+    while i < result.docs.len() {\\n+        if let d = result.docs.get(i) {\\n+            print(format_doc(&d))\\n+        }\\n+        i = i + 1\\n+    }\\n+}\\n+\\n+func driver_print_kinds() -> Int {\\n+    let kinds = token_kinds()\\n+    var i = 0\\n+    while i < kinds.len() {\\n+        if let k = kinds.get(i) {\\n+            print(kind_name(k))\\n+        }\\n+        i = i + 1\\n+    }\\n+    0\\n+}\\ndiff --git a/compiler/lex/README.md b/compiler/lex/README.md\\nnew file mode 100644\\nindex 00000000..daa8100a\\n--- /dev/null\\n+++ b/compiler/lex/README.md\\n@@ -0,0 +1,174 @@\\n+# sawlex: the Saw lexer, in Saw\\n+\\n+The lexer stage of the self-hosted compiler (`compiler/README.md`). It produces\\n+the same token kinds, token boundaries, 1-based `line:col` positions and lex-error\\n+positions as the Python lexer in `sawc/lexer.py`. `LANGUAGE_SPEC.md`'s lexical\\n+section is authoritative where the two disagree. The golden fixtures in\\n+`compiler/tests/lex/` pin its output.\\n+\\n+## Layout\\n+\\n+```\\n+compiler/lex/\\n+  Saw.toml          # package manifest (name = \\\"sawlex\\\")\\n+  src/lib.saw       # the token model (TokenKind, Token, StringSegment), `lex`, the dump format\\n+  tests/*.saw       # unit programs, one per token family\\n+```\\n+\\n+The CLI is the driver's `lex` subcommand:\\n+\\n+```\\n+./.venv/bin/python compiler/tools/build.py\\n+.build/sawc2 lex [--docs] <file.saw>\\n+.build/sawc2 lex --kinds\\n+```\\n+\\n+## Canonical token-dump format\\n+\\n+`sawc2 lex <file.saw>` emits one record per token, newline-separated:\\n+\\n+```\\n+KIND<TAB>line:col[<TAB>escaped-text][<TAB>suffix]\\n+```\\n+\\n+* `KIND` is the token kind's dump name (see the mapping table below).\\n+* `line:col` is 1-based, at the token's first byte or character.\\n+* `escaped-text` is the token's canonical text (decoded for strings; underscores\\n+  stripped and the base prefix lowercased for numbers), escaped at the **byte**\\n+  level: `\\\\\\\\` for backslash, `\\\\n` `\\\\t` `\\\\r` for those controls, `\\\\0` for NUL, any\\n+  other control byte (`< 0x20` or `0x7F`) as `\\\\xHH`, a space that ends the text\\n+  as `\\\\x20`, and every other byte, including raw UTF-8 `>= 0x80`, verbatim.\\n+* `suffix` is a 4th column, present **only** for a fixed-width-suffixed integer\\n+  literal: one of `i8`/`i16`/`i32`/`i64`/`u8`/`u16`/`u32`/`u64`. `255u8` dumps as\\n+  `INT<TAB>1:1<TAB>255<TAB>u8`; every other token stops at the escaped text.\\n+\\n+**No record ends in whitespace.** When the last field of a record is empty, it is\\n+left out together with the tab before it: the end-of-file token dumps as\\n+`EOF<TAB>6:1`, an empty string as `STRING<TAB>1:1`, and an empty doc line as\\n+`DOC<TAB>8:1<TAB>doc`. An empty field with another field after it keeps its tab,\\n+since that tab does not end the record. And a space that ends an escaped field\\n+is written `\\\\x20`, so `\\\"a \\\"` dumps as `STRING<TAB>1:1<TAB>a\\\\x20`. Tools that strip\\n+trailing whitespace, such as `git apply --whitespace=fix` and most editors,\\n+therefore leave every record intact.\\n+\\n+The format changed to this after the one-time comparison with the Python\\n+lexer's dump (SL-399). That comparison was made in the earlier format, where\\n+such a record ended in a tab or a space. The golden fixtures were regenerated in\\n+this format, and they are the oracle.\\n+\\n+`sawc2 lex --kinds` prints every kind's dump name, one per line, in the order\\n+`TokenKind` declares them.\\n+\\n+### An `INTERP_STRING` record carries its segments\\n+\\n+An interpolated string's `escaped-text` is the literal's **raw source spelling**\\n+between the quotes (undecoded), and the record then continues with **one field\\n+per typed segment** (design 268), in source order:\\n+\\n+```\\n+INTERP_STRING<TAB>line:col<TAB>raw<TAB>T:text<TAB>E:line:col:raw-expr...\\n+```\\n+\\n+* `T:` is a literal run, **decoded** (so an escaped brace is a plain `{`/`}`).\\n+* `E:line:col:` is an interpolation, carrying the **raw** text between its braces\\n+  and the exact 1-based source position of its opening `{`.\\n+\\n+Both are escaped as the text field is, final space included.\\n+\\n+An empty `T:` run is never emitted, so two adjacent interpolations produce two\\n+consecutive `E:` fields. No token carries both a suffix and segments, so the\\n+tail is unambiguous. The segments are what a parser consumes: nothing is encoded\\n+into the token's bytes.\\n+\\n+On a lex error the CLI emits a single record and exits 1:\\n+\\n+```\\n+ERROR<TAB>line:col<TAB>message\\n+```\\n+\\n+The position and the kind of error match the Python lexer. The message is this\\n+lexer's own wording.\\n+\\n+## Doc-comment trivia dump\\n+\\n+`sawc2 lex --docs <file.saw>` emits the documentation comments instead of the\\n+token stream, one record per captured line:\\n+\\n+```\\n+DOC<TAB>line:col<TAB>kind[<TAB>escaped-text]\\n+```\\n+\\n+* `kind` is `doc` for a `///` line and `module` for a `//!` line.\\n+* `line:col` is 1-based, at the leading `/`.\\n+* `escaped-text` is the line body with the `///`/`//!` marker and one following\\n+  space stripped, escaped by the same byte-level scheme as a token's text. An\\n+  empty body is left out with its tab.\\n+\\n+Doc comments are **trivia**: `lex` skips them exactly as it skips `//` comments,\\n+so the token dump above is unaffected. `lex` returns one `LexResult` carrying\\n+the tokens, the doc records and the string-segment arena together. There is no\\n+tokens-only entry point, because a token's `seg_start`/`seg_count` index into\\n+that arena. Only a comment that starts its line is a doc comment; `////`\\n+(four or more slashes) and a `///` trailing code on the same line are ordinary\\n+comments.\\n+\\n+## Kind-name mapping (TokenKind case to dump name)\\n+\\n+| TokenKind case | dump name | TokenKind case | dump name |\\n+|---|---|---|---|\\n+| `IntLit` | `INT` | `WrapAdd` | `WRAP_ADD` |\\n+| `FloatLit` | `FLOAT` | `WrapSub` | `WRAP_SUB` |\\n+| `StringLit` | `STRING` | `WrapMul` | `WRAP_MUL` |\\n+| `InterpString` | `INTERP_STRING` | `Not_` | `NOT` |\\n+| `Ident` | `IDENT` | `Move_` | `MOVE` |\\n+| `Func` | `FUNC` | `Unsafe_` | `UNSAFE` |\\n+| `Let` | `LET` | `Borrows` | `BORROWS` |\\n+| `Var` | `VAR` | `Lend` | `LEND` |\\n+| `If` | `IF` | `Assign` | `ASSIGN` |\\n+| `Else` | `ELSE` | `PlusAssign` | `PLUS_ASSIGN` |\\n+| `Guard` | `GUARD` | `MinusAssign` | `MINUS_ASSIGN` |\\n+| `Return` | `RETURN` | `StarAssign` | `STAR_ASSIGN` |\\n+| `True_` | `TRUE` | `SlashAssign` | `SLASH_ASSIGN` |\\n+| `False_` | `FALSE` | `PercentAssign` | `PERCENT_ASSIGN` |\\n+| `Struct` | `STRUCT` | `AmpAssign` | `AMP_ASSIGN` |\\n+| `Extension` | `EXTENSION` | `PipeAssign` | `PIPE_ASSIGN` |\\n+| `SelfKw` | `SELF` | `CaretAssign` | `CARET_ASSIGN` |\\n+| `Init` | `INIT` | `ShlAssign` | `SHL_ASSIGN` |\\n+| `NoneKw` | `NONE` | `ShrAssign` | `SHR_ASSIGN` |\\n+| `Enum` | `ENUM` | `Question` | `QUESTION` |\\n+| `Case` | `CASE` | `DoubleQuestion` | `DOUBLE_QUESTION` |\\n+| `Match` | `MATCH` | `Exclaim` | `EXCLAIM` |\\n+| `While` | `WHILE` | `QuestionDot` | `QUESTION_DOT` |\\n+| `Break` | `BREAK` | `DotDot` | `DOTDOT` |\\n+| `Continue` | `CONTINUE` | `DotDotEq` | `DOTDOT_EQ` |\\n+| `Trait` | `TRAIT` | `Ellipsis` | `ELLIPSIS` |\\n+| `For` | `FOR` | `LParen` | `LPAREN` |\\n+| `In` | `IN` | `RParen` | `RPAREN` |\\n+| `Extern` | `EXTERN` | `LBrace` | `LBRACE` |\\n+| `As` | `AS` | `RBrace` | `RBRACE` |\\n+| `Try` | `TRY` | `LBracket` | `LBRACKET` |\\n+| `Catch` | `CATCH` | `RBracket` | `RBRACKET` |\\n+| `Static` | `STATIC` | `Comma` | `COMMA` |\\n+| `Public` | `PUBLIC` | `Colon` | `COLON` |\\n+| `Plus` … `Tilde` | `PLUS` … `TILDE` | `Semicolon` | `SEMICOLON` |\\n+| `HashDirective` | `HASH_DIRECTIVE` | `Arrow` | `ARROW` |\\n+| `DollarParam` | `DOLLAR_PARAM` | `Dot` | `DOT` |\\n+| `Newline` | `NEWLINE` | `At` | `AT` |\\n+| `Eof` | `EOF` | | |\\n+\\n+The remaining single-character operators map by uppercasing:\\n+`Minus`→`MINUS`, `Star`→`STAR`, `Slash`→`SLASH`, `Percent`→`PERCENT`,\\n+`Eq`→`EQ`, `Neq`→`NEQ`, `Lt`→`LT`, `Gt`→`GT`, `Lte`→`LTE`, `Gte`→`GTE`,\\n+`And`→`AND`, `Or`→`OR`, `Ampersand`→`AMPERSAND`, `Pipe`→`PIPE`,\\n+`Caret`→`CARET`, `Tilde`→`TILDE`. The words `module`, `import`, `export`,\\n+`package`, `parent` and `type` are not keywords: each lexes as `IDENT`, and the\\n+parser decides by position.\\n+\\n+## How it reads the source\\n+\\n+The lexer works over the source's UTF-8 **bytes** while counting columns by code\\n+point (a column advances once per non-continuation byte). That gives the\\n+per-code-point columns positions are defined in, and lets token text be\\n+assembled from raw bytes. The only place a scalar is re-encoded is a `\\\\u{...}`\\n+escape, which goes through the std `StringBuilder.append(scalar: Scalar)`\\n+(design 245).\\ndiff --git a/selfhost/lexer/Saw.toml b/compiler/lex/Saw.toml\\nsimilarity index 100%\\nrename from selfhost/lexer/Saw.toml\\nrename to compiler/lex/Saw.toml\\ndiff --git a/selfhost/lexer/src/lib.saw b/compiler/lex/src/lib.saw\\nsimilarity index 88%\\nrename from selfhost/lexer/src/lib.saw\\nrename to compiler/lex/src/lib.saw\\nindex c7d4da20..ba67aeb4 100644\\n--- a/selfhost/lexer/src/lib.saw\\n+++ b/compiler/lex/src/lib.saw\\n@@ -1,12 +1,11 @@\\n-// sawlex — the Saw language lexer, ported to Saw (design 116, the self-hosting\\n-// pilot). This is a faithful port of `sawc/lexer.py`: same token kinds, same\\n-// token boundaries, same 1-based line:col positions, same lex-error positions.\\n+// sawlex, the lexer stage of the self-hosted compiler. Its token kinds, token\\n+// boundaries, 1-based line:col positions and lex-error positions match\\n+// `sawc/lexer.py`, the frozen compiler's lexer; the golden fixtures in\\n+// compiler/tests/lex pin them.\\n //\\n-// The port lexes over the source's UTF-8 BYTES while counting columns by\\n-// Unicode code points (a column advances once per non-continuation byte). That\\n-// reproduces the Python lexer's per-code-point column counting exactly while\\n-// letting token text be assembled from raw bytes — no scalar re-encoding needed\\n-// except for `\\\\u{...}` escapes, which are UTF-8-encoded here.\\n+// It lexes over the source's UTF-8 BYTES while counting columns by Unicode code\\n+// points (a column advances once per non-continuation byte), so token text is\\n+// assembled from raw bytes and only a `\\\\u{...}` escape re-encodes a scalar.\\n //\\n // See README.md for the canonical dump format and the kind-name mapping table.\\n \\n@@ -125,8 +124,10 @@ func char_str(u: Int) -> String {\\n }\\n \\n // =============================================================================\\n-// Token model — mirrors sawc/lexer.py's TokenType. Its text is empty, so its record ends at the position.\\n     let e = lexed(\\\"x\\\")\\n     assert(kind(&e, 1) == TokenKind.Eof, \\\"EOF terminator\\\")\\n+    assert(at(&e, 1).equals(\\\"EOF\\\\t1:2\\\"), \\\"EOF record has no text field\\\")\\n \\n     print(\\\"operators: ok\\\")\\n }\\ndiff --git a/selfhost/lexer/tests/ranges.saw b/compiler/lex/tests/ranges.saw\\nsimilarity index 96%\\nrename from selfhost/lexer/tests/ranges.saw\\nrename to compiler/lex/tests/ranges.saw\\nindex 42e6acd2..db412bec 100644\\n--- a/selfhost/lexer/tests/ranges.saw\\n+++ b/compiler/lex/tests/ranges.saw\\n@@ -1,9 +1,10 @@\\n // Range operators vs float dots, including design 161's rule that a number's\\n // `.` needs a digit after it.\\n-import src.lib.*\\n+import src.lib.{LexResult, format_token, lex}\\n \\n func lexed(s: String) -> LexResult {\\n-    match lex(s) {\\n+    let result = lex(s)\\n+    match result {\\n         case Ok(r) -> move r,\\n         case Err(e) -> panic(\\\"unexpected lex error at {e.line}:{e.col}: {e.message}\\\")\\n     }\\ndiff --git a/compiler/tests/lex/borrowing_producer_every_path.saw b/compiler/tests/lex/borrowing_producer_every_path.saw\\nnew file mode 100644\\nindex 00000000..d15d3cd7\\n--- /dev/null\\n+++ b/compiler/tests/lex/borrowing_producer_every_path.saw\\n@@ -0,0 +1,81 @@\\n+// Fixture: a tracked file, copied whole. Taken from examples/borrowing_producer_every_path.saw.\\n+// K144 accept twin (design 275 U3, codex r1 #1): the origin check follows\\n+// EVERY returning path, so a producer whose every leaf — a `return`, each\\n+// `match` arm, and each arm of an `if` nested in one — constructs the struct\\n+// with `lends self` is accepted, and each spelling produces a working window.\\n+//\\n+// K143 CONTROLS (codex r4 #1): the borrowing walk descends a function type's\\n+// return and parameters, and a callable that names NO borrowing struct is\\n+// untouched by it — a reference in a closure PARAMETER stays legal (that is\\n+// the no-escape walk's exemption, which the borrowing walk does not share),\\n+// and a closure literal inside a window body is an ordinary closure.\\n+// EXPECT: success\\n+// EXPECT-OUTPUT:\\n+// return path 6\\n+// match arm 6\\n+// nested if 5\\n+// total 17\\n+// callable controls 3 12\\n+\\n+struct Bag {\\n+    private items: Vector<Int>\\n+}\\n+extension Bag: NoCopy {}\\n+\\n+borrows struct BagIterator {\\n+    private bag: &Bag\\n+    private index: Int\\n+}\\n+\\n+extension Bag {\\n+    public func pick(&self, k: Int, from_one: Bool) borrows -> BagIterator {\\n+        if k == 0 { return BagIterator(bag: lends self, index: 0) }\\n+        match k {\\n+            case 1 -> BagIterator(bag: lends self, index: 0),\\n+            case _ -> {\\n+                if from_one { BagIterator(bag: lends self, index: 1) }\\n+                else { BagIterator(bag: lends self, index: 0) }\\n+            }\\n+        }\\n+    }\\n+}\\n+\\n+extension BagIterator: Iterator {\\n+    type Item = Int\\n+    public func next(&var self) -> Int? {\\n+        if self.index < self.bag.items.len() {\\n+            let i = self.index\\n+            self.index = self.index + 1\\n+            self.bag.items.get(i)\\n+        } else {\\n+            None\\n+        }\\n+    }\\n+}\\n+\\n+func sum(b: &Bag, k: Int, from_one: Bool) -> Int {\\n+    var total = 0\\n+    for x in b.pick(k, from_one) { total = total + x }\\n+    total\\n+}\\n+\\n+// A reference in a closure parameter: legal, and the borrowing walk that now\\n+// descends `(&Bag) sync -> Int` finds no borrowing struct in it.\\n+func through(b: &Bag, body: (&Bag) sync -> Int) -> Int { body(&b) }\\n+\\n+func main() {\\n+    var v: Vector<Int> = [1, 2, 3]\\n+    let bag = Bag(items: move v)\\n+    let a = sum(&bag, 0, false)\\n+    let b = sum(&bag, 1, false)\\n+    let c = sum(&bag, 2, true)\\n+    print(\\\"return path {a}\\\")\\n+    print(\\\"match arm {b}\\\")\\n+    print(\\\"nested if {c}\\\")\\n+    print(\\\"total {a + b + c}\\\")\\n+    let d = through(&bag, { g in g.items.len() })\\n+    let twice = { n: Int in n * 2 }\\n+    var e = 0\\n+    for x in bag.pick(0, false) { e = e + twice(x) }\\n+    print(\\\"callable controls {d} {e}\\\")\\n+}\\ndiff --git a/compiler/tests/lex/borrowing_producer_every_path.tokens Taken from examples/vector_in_erased_result.saw.\\n+// EXPECT: success\\n+// EXPECT-OUTPUT:\\n+// 2\\n+// a\\n+// b\\n+// err7\\n+\\n+// Regression (design 67 / dogfood DF6): a generic type with a DEFAULT type param\\n+// used with fewer args (`Vector<Dependency>` = `Vector<Dependency, GlobalAllocator>`) must\\n+// fill that default consistently BEFORE mangling, including when NESTED inside\\n+// another generic (a `Result` Ok type). Previously the default-arg filling\\n+// diverged — `Vector$1$Dependency` in one path, `Vector$2$Dependency$GlobalAllocator` in\\n+// another — so `Result<Vector<Dependency>, Box<any Error>>` registered the enum\\n+// under one mangled name and looked it up under the other, ICE'ing the err-return\\n+// path (`KeyError: Result$...Vector$1$Dependency...`). Now the arities agree.\\n+\\n+struct Dependency {\\n+    name: String\\n+}\\n+\\n+struct MyError {\\n+    code: Int\\n+}\\n+\\n+extension MyError: Error {\\n+    func format(&self, into: &var StringBuilder) {\\n+        try! into.append(\\\"err\\\")\\n+        try! into.append(self.code)\\n+    }\\n+}\\n+\\n+// Ok type `Vector<Dependency>` (arity-1 written), erased-error Result.\\n+func build(ok: Bool) -> Result<Vector<Dependency>, Box<any Error>> {\\n+    if not ok {\\n+        return MyError(code: 7)\\n+    }\\n+    var v = Vector<Dependency>()\\n+    try! v.push(Dependency(name: \\\"a\\\"))\\n+    try! v.push(Dependency(name: \\\"b\\\"))\\n+    return move v\\n+}\\n+\\n+func main() {\\n+    match build(true) {\\n+        case Ok(v) -> {\\n+            print(v.len())\\n+            var i = 0\\n+            while i < v.len() {\\n+                guard let d = v.get(i) else { break }\\n+                print(d.name)\\n+                i = i + 1\\n+            }\\n+        },\\n+        case Err(e) -> print(\\\"{e}\\\")\\n+    }\\n+    match build(false) {\\n+        case Ok(v) -> print(v.len()),\\n+        case Err(e) -> print(\\\"{e}\\\")\\n+    }\\n+}\\ndiff --git self.counts[key] = self.counts.get(key, 0) + n\\n+\\n+\\n+def unit_programs():\\n+    \\\"\\\"\\\"`compiler/<stage>/tests/*.saw`, as (name, path), sorted.\\\"\\\"\\\"\\n+    out = []\\n+    for path build_and_run_unit(name, path):\\n+    \\\"\\\"\\\"(name, failure text or None).\\\"\\\"\\\"\\n+    exe = os.path.join(UNIT_OUT, name.replace(\\\"/\\\", \\\"_\\\"))\\n+    ok, output = build.build_program(path, exe)\\n+    if not ok:\\n+        return name, \\\"unit %s: does not compile: %s\\\" % (name, _first_line(output))\\n+    try:\\n+        r = subprocess.run([exe], capture_output=True, text=True, timeout=RUN_TIMEOUT)\\n+    except subprocess.TimeoutExpired:\\n+        return name, \\\"unit %s: timed out\\\" % name\\n+    if r.returncode != 0:\\n+        detail = cover.\\\"\\\"\\\"\\n+    sources = sorted(glob.glob(os.path.join(LEX_FIXTURES, \\\"*.saw\\\")))\\n+    expectations = sorted(glob.glob(os.path.join(LEX_FIXTURES, \\\"*.tokens\\\"))\\n+                          + glob.glob(os.path.join(LEX_FIXTURES, \\\"*.docs\\\")))\\n+    stems = {os.path.splitext(p)[0] for p in sources}\\n+    for exp in expectations:\\n+        if os.path.splitext(exp)[0] not in stems:\\n+            run.fail(\\\"golden %s: no input beside it\\\" % os.path.relpath(exp, REPO))\\n+    if not sources:\\n+        run.fail(\\\"golden: no fixtures in %s\\\" % ((\\\".tokens\\\", ()), (\\\".docs\\\", (\\\"--docs\\\",))):\\n+            exp_path = stem + suffix\\n+            if not os.path.exists(exp_path):\\n+                continue\\n+            with open(exp_path, \\\"rb\\\") as fh:\\n+                expected = fh.read()\\n+            code, got = sawc2(\\\"lex\\\", *flags, src)\\n+            want_code = 1 if expected.startswith(b\\\"ERROR\\\\t\\\") else 0\\n+            if got != expected:\\n+                run.fail(\\\"golden %s%s: output differs at %s\\\"\\n+                         % (rel, \\\" --docs\\\" if flags else \\\"\\\", _first_difference(expected, got)))\\n+            elif code != want_code:\\n+                run.fail(\\\"golden %s%s: exit %d, expected %d\\\"\\n+ in expected.split(b\\\"\\\\n\\\"):\\n+                    fields = record.split(b\\\"\\\\t\\\")\\n+                    if fields[0] == b\\\"ERROR\\\" and len(fields) >= 3:\\n+                        errors_seen.add(fields[2].decode(\\\"utf-8\\\"))\\n+                    elif fields[0]:\\n+                        kinds_seen.add(fields[0].decode(\\\"ascii\\\"))\\n+        run.count(\\\"golden fixtures\\\")\\n+    check_kind_coverage(run, kinds_seen)\\n+    check_error_coverage(run, errors_seen)\\n+\\n+\\n+def _first_difference(expected, got):\\n+    exp_lines = expected.split(b\\\"\\\\n\\\")\\n+    got_lines = got.split(b\\\"\\\\n\\\")\\n+    for i in range(max(len(exp_lines), len(got_lines))):\\n+        a = exp_lines[i] if i < len(exp_lines) else b\\\"<end>\\\"\\n+        b = got_lines[i] if i < len(got_lines) else b\\\"<end>\\\"\\n+        if a != b:\\n+            return \\\"record %d: expected %r, got %r\\\" % (i + 1, a[:80], b[:80])\\n+    return \\\"a trailing byte\\\"\\n+\\n+\\n+def lexer_program():\\n+    src = subset_check.SourceFile(LEXER_SOURCE)\\n+    if src.program is None:\\n+        raise RuntimeError(\\\"cannot parse %s\\\" % os.path.relpath(LEXER_SOURCE, REPO))\\n+    return src.program\\n+\\n+\\n+def check_kind_coverage(run, kinds_seen):\\n+    \\\"\\\"\\\"The kinds come from the lexer: `sawc2 lex --kinds`, whose length but TokenKind \\\"\\n+                 \\\"declares %d cases\\\" % (len(set(kinds)), len(kinds), cases))\\n+    for kind in kinds:\\n+        if kind not in kinds_seen:\\n+            run.fail(\\\"coverage: token kind %s appears in no golden fixture\\\" % kind)\\n+    run.count(\\\"token kinds\\\", len(kinds))\\n+\\n+\\n+def check_error_coverage(run, errors_seen):\\n+ .build/sawc2\\n+    python compiler/tools/build.py ENTRY.saw -o OUT  # any program over the stages\\n+\\n+The interpreter running this script runs `sawc/sawc.py`, so invoke it with the\\n+venv's Python. `STAGE_PACKAGES` is the one place that maps a stage package to\\n+its directory: the driver, the unit programs and the runner all build through\\n+`build_program`, so a new stage is registered here once.\\n+\\\"\\\"\\\"\\n+import argparse\\n+import os\\n+import subprocess\\n+import sys\\n+\\n+REPO = os.path.dirname(os.path.dirname(os.path.dirname(os.path.abspath(__file__))))\\n+FROZEN_SAWC = os.path.join(REPO, \\\"sawc\\\", \\\"sawc.py\\\")\\n+BUILD_DIR = os.path.join(REPO, \\\".build\\\")\\n+SAWC2 = os.path.join(BUILD_DIR, \\\"sawc2\\\")\\n+DRIVER_ENTRY = os.path.join(REPO, \\\"compiler\\\", \\\"driver\\\", \\\"src\\\", \\\"main.saw\\\")\\n+\\n+# (package name, directory relative to the repo root). Each stage is a package\\n+# the others import as `<name>.src.<module>`.\\n+STAGE_PACKAGES = (\\n+    (\\\"sawlex\\\", \\\"compiler/lex\\\"),\\n+)\\n+\\n+\\n+def build_program(entry, out):\\n+    \\\"\\\"\\\"Compile `entry` to the executable `out`. Returns (ok, compiler output).\\\"\\\"\\\"\\n+    os.makedirs(os.path.dirname(out), exist_ok=True)\\n+    argv = [sys.executable, FROZEN_SAWC, entry, \\\"-o\\\", out]\\n+    for name, rel in STAGE_PACKAGES:\\n+        argv += [\\\"--module-path\\\", \\\"%s=%s\\\" % (name, os.path.join(REPO, rel))]\\n+    r = subprocess.run(argv, cwd=REPO, capture_output=True, text=True)\\n+    return r.returncode == 0, (r.stdout + r.stderr).strip()\\n+\\n+\\n+def build_sawc2():\\n+    \\\"\\\"\\\"Build the driver to `.build/sawc2`. Returns (ok, compiler output).\\\"\\\"\\\"\\n+    return build_program(DRIVER_ENTRY, SAWC2)\\n+\\n+\\n+def main():\\n+    ap = argparse.ArgumentParser(description=__doc__.splitlines()[0])\\n+    ap.add_argument(\\\"entry\\\", nargs=\\\"?\\\", help=\\\"a program to build instead of sawc2\\\")\\n+    ap.add_argument(\\\"-o\\\", dest=\\\"out\\\", help=\\\"the output path (required with ENTRY)\\\")\\n+    args = ap.parse_args()\\n+    if args.entry is None:\\n+        ok, output = build_sawc2()\\n+        target = SAWC2\\n+    else:\\n+        if args.out is None:\\n+            ap.error(\\\"-o is required when an ENTRY is given\\\")\\n+        ok, output = build_program(os.path.abspath(args.entry), os.path.abspath(args.out))\\n+        target = args.out\\n+    if output:\\n+        print(output)\\n+    if not ok:\\n+        print(\\\"build: FAILED: %s\\\" % target)\\n+        return 1\\n+    print(\\\"build: %s\\\" % os.path.relpath(target, REPO))\\n+    return 0\\n+\\n+\\n+if __name__ == \\\"__main__\\\":\\n+    sys.exit(main())\\ndiff --git a/compiler/tools/subset_check.py b/compiler/tools/subset_check.py\\nnew file mode 100644\\nindex 00000000..8b3acd86\\n--- /dev/null\\n+++ b/compiler/tools/subset_check.py\\n@@ -0,0 +1,2140 @@\\n+#!/usr/bin/env python3\\n+\\\"\\\"\\\"Refuse, in the self-hosted compiler's source, the shapes Stage 0 mishandles.\\n+\\n+Each rule in `RULES` enforces one subset rule or one checkable hazard entry and\\n+names that source in its diagnostic. The frozen compiler's own lexer and parser\\n+read the source, so the checker sees exactly what Stage 0 reads.\\n+\\n+    python compiler/tools/subset_check.py          # compiler/**/*.saw, no tests/ or sidecars\\n+    python compiler/tools/subset_check.py FILE...  # each file as a unit program\\n+\\\"\\\"\\\"\\n+import dataclasses\\n+import glob\\n+import os\\n+import re\\n+import sys\\n+\\n+REPO = os.path.dirname(os.path.dirname(os.path.dirname(os.path.abspath(__file__))))\\n+sys.path.insert(0, os.path.join(REPO, \\\"sawc\\\"))\\n+sys.path.insert(0, os.path.dirname(os.path.abspath(__file__)))\\n+\\n+import ast_nodes as A  # noqa: E402\\n+from lexer import Lexer, TokenType as T  # noqa: E402\\n+from parser import Parser  # noqa: E402\\n+from build import STAGE_PACKAGES  # noqa: E402\\n+\\n+ARCH = \\\"SL:architecture §4\\\"\\n+\\n+RULES = {\\n+    \\\"lex\\\": ARCH + \\\": the frozen lexer must accept the source\\\",\\n+    \\\"parse\\\": ARCH + \\\": the frozen parser must accept the source\\\",\\n+    \\\"file-end\\\": \\\"SL:hazards C2\\\",\\n+    \\\"sync-only\\\": ARCH,\\n+    \\\"closure-capture\\\": ARCH,\\n+    \\\"type-alias\\\": ARCH + \\\"; SL:hazards S9\\\",\\n+    \\\"prelude-type-name\\\": ARCH + \\\"; SL:hazards L17\\\",\\n+    \\\"selective-imports\\\": ARCH + \\\"; SL:hazards L3\\\",\\n+    \\\"import-allowlist\\\": ARCH + \\\": the compiler's std cone\\\",\\n+    \\\"std-api\\\": ARCH + \\\": the API allowlist\\\",\\n+    \\\"borrows-accessor\\\": ARCH + \\\": accessors belong to the new std\\\",\\n+    \\\"generic-extension-init\\\": ARCH + \\\"; SL:hazards S1\\\",\\n+    \\\"integer-overload\\\": ARCH + \\\"; SL:hazards L1\\\",\\n+    \\\"any-type\\\": ARCH + \\\"; SL:hazards S12\\\",\\n+    \\\"box-type\\\": ARCH + \\\"; SL:hazards L8\\\",\\n+    \\\"cell-type\\\": ARCH + \\\"; SL:hazards S11, S15\\\",\\n+    \\\"raw-pointer\\\": ARCH + \\\"; SL:hazards C1\\\",\\n+    \\\"fixed-array\\\": ARCH + \\\"; SL:hazards L7\\\",\\n+    \\\"value-loop\\\": ARCH + \\\"; SL:hazards L11\\\",\\n+    \\\"statement-arm\\\": ARCH + \\\"; SL:hazards L13, C4\\\",\\n+    \\\"borrow-syntax\\\": ARCH,\\n+    \\\"test-directive\\\": ARCH,\\n+    \\\"deinit-body\\\": \\\"SL:hazards: leaks are tolerated in Stage 1\\\",\\n+    \\\"borrowed-match-payload\\\": \\\"SL:hazards S2\\\",\\n+    \\\"root-reuse\\\": \\\"SL:hazards S4\\\",\\n+    \\\"var-ref-into-let\\\": \\\"SL:hazards S5\\\",\\n+    \\\"function-exit\\\": \\\"SL:hazards S6\\\",\\n+    \\\"int-literal-range\\\": \\\"SL:hazards S7\\\",\\n+    \\\"enum-equatable-body\\\": \\\"SL:hazards S8\\\",\\n+    \\\"nested-optional\\\": \\\"SL:hazards S10\\\",\\n+    \\\"argument-labels\\\": \\\"SL:hazards S14\\\",\\n+    \\\"index-receiver-call\\\": \\\"SL:hazards S15\\\",\\n+    \\\"cast-then-optional\\\": \\\"SL:hazards S17\\\",\\n+    \\\"program-unique-names\\\": \\\"SL:hazards S18\\\",\\n+    \\\"written-type-shape\\\": ARCH + \\\"; SL:hazards S19\\\",\\n+    \\\"float-literal\\\": ARCH + \\\"; SL:hazards S20\\\",\\n+    \\\"interpolation-line-break\\\": \\\"SL:hazards S21\\\",\\n+    \\\"inline-module\\\": \\\"SL:hazards S22\\\",\\n+    \\\"from-raw-literal\\\": \\\"SL:hazards L1\\\",\\n+    \\\"closure-syntax\\\": \\\"SL:hazards L2\\\",\\n+    \\\"default-value-literal\\\": \\\"SL:hazards L3\\\",\\n+    \\\"optional-shaping\\\": \\\"SL:hazards L4\\\",\\n+    \\\"empty-struct\\\": \\\"SL:hazards L5\\\",\\n+    \\\"bounded-extension\\\": \\\"SL:hazards L6\\\",\\n+    \\\"name-collision\\\": \\\"SL:hazards L9\\\",\\n+    \\\"type-param-receiver\\\": \\\"SL:hazards L10\\\",\\n+    \\\"leading-minus\\\": \\\"SL:hazards L12\\\",\\n+    \\\"nesting-depth\\\": \\\"SL:hazards L14\\\",\\n+    \\\"chain-length\\\": \\\"SL:hazards L14\\\",\\n+    \\\"generic-extension-params\\\": \\\"SL:hazards L18\\\",\\n+    \\\"interpolation-content\\\": \\\"SL:hazards C3\\\",\\n+}\\n+\\n+# The std modules the compiler may import. The bootstrap std unit must build\\n+# every module on this list, so each addition widens that unit's cone. Stage 1\\n+# may skip drops, so an addition also re-checks that every std `deinit` it\\n+# reaches only frees memory or closes a descriptor (SL:hazards, leak tolerance).\\n+ALLOWED_STD_MODULES = (\\\"env\\\", \\\"file\\\", \\\"path\\\")\\n+\\n+PACKAGE_RELATIVE_HEADS = (\\\"src\\\", \\\"parent\\\", \\\"package\\\")\\n+\\n+# The std members the compiler may call, each meaning the same under `sawc/std`\\n+# (Stage 0) and the new std (Stage 1): a free function or constructor by name, a\\n+# static member as `Type.member`, an instance method as `.name`. An addition\\n+# re-checks the `deinit` condition `ALLOWED_STD_MODULES` states.\\n+STD_API = (\\n+    \\\"print\\\", \\\"panic\\\", \\\"assert\\\",\\n+    \\\"Byte\\\", \\\"Path\\\", \\\"Scalar\\\", \\\"StringBuilder\\\", \\\"Vector\\\",\\n+    \\\"Env.args\\\", \\\"File.open\\\", \\\"UInt8.from\\\", \\\"Result.Ok\\\", \\\"Result.Err\\\",\\n+    \\\".append\\\", \\\".build\\\", \\\".byte_at\\\", \\\".clear\\\", \\\".equals\\\", \\\".get\\\", \\\".is_empty\\\", \\\".len\\\",\\n+    \\\".push\\\", \\\".read\\\", \\\".starts_with\\\", \\\".substring\\\", \\\".to_string\\\", \\\".to_uint\\\",\\n+)\\n+\\n+# Std calls the lockdown retires, refused by name whatever the allowlist says.\\n+RETIRED_STD_METHODS = (\\\"with_ref\\\", \\\"with_var_ref\\\", \\\"with_unique\\\")\\n+\\n+SYNC_ONLY_NAMES = (\\\"TaskGroup\\\", \\\"Task\\\", \\\"VoidTask\\\", \\\"Thread\\\", \\\"VoidThread\\\", \\\"Channel\\\",\\n+                   \\\"sleep\\\", \\\"cancelled\\\", \\\"yield_now\\\", \\\"dump_tasks\\\", \\\"spawn\\\")\\n+CELL_NAMES = (\\\"Mutex\\\", \\\"SpinLock\\\", \\\"Once\\\", \\\"UnsafeMutableInterior\\\", \\\"Atomic\\\", \\\"Arc\\\")\\n+# The frozen parser's raw-pointer spellings. The other unsafe types are the\\n+# `unsafe struct`s std declares, which `StdFacts` collects.\\n+POINTER_TYPE_NAMES = (\\\"UnsafePointer\\\", \\\"UnsafeConstPointer\\\")\\n+COLLECTIONS = (\\\"Vector\\\", \\\"Map\\\", \\\"Set\\\")\\n+NON_INT_INTEGERS = (A.TypeKind.UINT, A.TypeKind.INT8, A.TypeKind.INT16, A.TypeKind.INT32,\\n+                    A.TypeKind.INT64, A.TypeKind.UINT8, A.TypeKind.UINT16,\\n+                    A.TypeKind.UINT32, A.TypeKind.UINT64)\\n+UNSIGNED_64 = (A.TypeKind.UINT, A.TypeKind.UINT64)\\n+INT_MAX = (1 << 63) - 1\\n+\\n+# Methods a call may reach through an indexed receiver or a `get` result: each\\n+# only reads it, so a copy of the element gives the same answer.\\n+READ_ONLY_METHODS = (\\\"byte_at\\\", \\\"compare\\\", \\\"contains\\\", \\\"contains_key\\\", \\\"copy\\\", \\\"ends_with\\\",\\n+                     \\\"equals\\\", \\\"get\\\", \\\"hash\\\", \\\"index_of\\\", \\\"is_empty\\\", \\\"is_none\\\", \\\"is_some\\\",\\n+                     \\\"len\\\", \\\"starts_with\\\", \\\"substring\\\", \\\"to_string\\\")\\n+\\n+MAX_NESTING = 30\\n+MAX_CHAIN = 100\\n+# A written type nests fewer levels than the lowest depth bound among the\\n+# frozen compiler's type walks (SL:hazards S19).\\n+MAX_TYPE_DEPTH = 7\\n+\\n+STATEMENT_KEYWORDS = (T.RETURN, T.BREAK, T.CONTINUE, T.LET, T.VAR, T.GUARD, T.WHILE, T.FOR)\\n+# The tokens that can end an operand. A `*` or `-` after one, across any newline\\n+# the parser skips, is a binary operator; after anything else it is a prefix\\n+# operator, except that `.*` is an import glob.\\n+OPERAND_END = (T.IDENT, T.INT, T.FLOAT, T.STRING, T.INTERP_STRING, T.RPAREN, T.RBRACKET,\\n+               T.RBRACE, T.SELF, T.TRUE, T.FALSE, T.NONE, T.EXCLAIM, T.QUESTION,\\n+               T.HASH_DIRECTIVE, T.DOLLAR_PARAM)\\n+\\n+_POS_RE = re.compile(r\\\"at (\\\\d+):(\\\\d+)(: )?\\\")\\n+\\n+\\n+@dataclasses.dataclass(frozen=True, order=True)\\n+class Diagnostic:\\n+    path: str\\n+    line: int\\n+    rule: str\\n+    message: str\\n+\\n+    def render(self):\\n+        return \\\"%s:%d: %s: %s (%s)\\\" % (self.path, self.line, self.rule, self.message,\\n+                                       RULES[self.rule])\\n+\\n+\\n+class SourceFile:\\n+    \\\"\\\"\\\"One file: its text, the frozen lexer's tokens and the frozen parser's tree.\\\"\\\"\\\"\\n+\\n+    def __init__(self, path):\\n+        self.path = path\\n+        self.rel = os.path.relpath(path, REPO)\\n+        with open(path, encoding=\\\"utf-8\\\") as fh:\\n+            self.text = fh.read()\\n+        self.tokens = None\\n+        self.program = None\\n+        self.lex_error = None\\n+        self.parse_error = None\\n+        lexer = Lexer(self.text)\\n+        try:\\n+            self.tokens = lexer.tokenize()\\n+        except Exception as exc:  # the frozen lexer raises more than SyntaxError\\n+            self.lex_error = _position(exc)\\n+            return\\n+        try:\\n+            self.program = Parser(self.tokens, source_file=path,\\n+                                  doc_comments=lexer.doc_comments).parse()\\n+        except Exception as exc:\\n+            self.parse_error = _position(exc)\\n+\\n+\\n+def _position(exc):\\n+    \\\"\\\"\\\"The line and message of a frozen lexer or parser error. The first\\n+    position is the file's: an error inside an interpolation nests the inner\\n+    lexer's, whose line 1 is the interpolation's, after it.\\\"\\\"\\\"\\n+    text = str(exc) or type(exc).__name__\\n+    found = list(_POS_RE.finditer(text))\\n+    if not found:\\n+        return 1, \\\"%s: %s\\\" % (type(exc).__name__, text.split(\\\"\\\\n\\\")[0])\\n+    first = found[0]\\n+    if first.group(3):\\n+        return int(first.group(1)), text[first.end():].split(\\\"\\\\n\\\")[0]\\n+    message = text[:first.start()].strip()\\n+    for inner in found[1:]:\\n+        if inner.group(3):\\n+            message += \\\": \\\" + text[inner.end():].split(\\\"\\\\n\\\")[0]\\n+            break\\n+    return int(first.group(1)), message\\n+\\n+\\n+def programs(prog):\\n+    \\\"\\\"\\\"`prog` and every inline `module m { ... }` body inside it, outermost first.\\\"\\\"\\\"\\n+    stack = [prog]\\n+    while stack:\\n+        p = stack.pop()\\n+        yield p\\n+        for md in reversed(p.module_decls):\\n+            if md.body is not None:\\n+                stack.append(md.body)\\n+\\n+\\n+# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\\n+# Facts about std and the builtins, which share the program with the compiler.\\n+# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\\n+\\n+class StdFacts:\\n+    def __init__(self):\\n+        from mono_identity import PRIMITIVE_TYPE_NAMES\\n+        self.names = {}\\n+        self.generic_functions = set()\\n+        self.generic_methods = set()\\n+        self.types = set(PRIMITIVE_TYPE_NAMES) | {\\\"Void\\\", \\\"Never\\\"}\\n+        # Names a compiler type may not take: every type the builtins or a std\\n+        # module make public, gated or not (SL:hazards L17).\\n+        self.reserved_types = set(self.types)\\n+        self.methods = set()\\n+        self.borrows_methods = set()\\n+        self.unsafe_types = set(POINTER_TYPE_NAMES)\\n+        self.generic_types = set()\\n+        self.method_kinds = {}\\n+        std_dir = os.path.join(REPO, \\\"sawc\\\", \\\"std\\\")\\n+        paths = sorted(glob.glob(os.path.join(std_dir, \\\"**\\\", \\\"*.saw\\\"), recursive=True))\\n+        paths.append(os.path.join(REPO, \\\"sawc\\\", \\\"builtin.saw\\\"))\\n+        for path in paths:\\n+            src = SourceFile(path)\\n+            if src.program is None:\\n+                raise RuntimeError(\\\"subset_check: cannot parse %s\\\" % src.rel)\\n+            builtin = path.endswith(\\\"builtin.saw\\\")\\n+            for prog in programs(src.program):\\n+                self._collect(prog, src.rel, builtin)\\n+\\n+    def _collect(self, prog, rel, builtin):\\n+        for fn in prog.functions:\\n+            self.names.setdefault(fn.name, rel)\\n+            if fn.type_params:\\n+                self.generic_functions.add(fn.name)\\n+        for st in prog.statics:\\n+            self.names.setdefault(st.name, rel)\\n+        for decl in prog.structs + prog.enums + prog.traits + prog.type_definitions:\\n+            self.types.add(decl.name)\\n+            if builtin or decl.visibility != A.Visibility.PRIVATE:\\n+                self.reserved_types.add(decl.name)\\n+        for s in prog.structs:\\n+            if s.is_unsafe:\\n+                self.unsafe_types.add(s.name)\\n+        for decl in prog.structs + prog.enums:\\n+            if decl.type_params:\\n+                self.generic_types.add(decl.name)\\n+        for tr in prog.traits:\\n+            self.methods.update(m.name for m in tr.methods)\\n+        for ext in prog.extensions:\\n+            for m in ext.methods:\\n+                self.methods.add(m.name)\\n+                if m.type_params or ext.type_params:\\n+                    self.generic_methods.add(m.name)\\n+                if in walk(self.src.program):\\n+            if isinstance(n, A.IntLiteral) and n.suffix is None \\\\\\n+                    and isinstance(n.value, int) and n.value > INT_MAX \\\\\\n+                    and id(n) not in allowed:\\n+                self.report(n.line, \\\"int-literal-range\\\",\\n+                            \\\"an unsuffixed integer literal above Int.max wraps; give it a \\\"\\n+                            \\\"suffix or a `UInt64` binding\\\")\\n+            elif isinstance(n, A.FloatLiteral):\\n+                self.report(n.line, \\\"float-literal\\\", \\\"the subset has no float literals\\\")\\n+\\n+    # -- declarations and bodies ---------------------------------------------\\n+\\n+    def check_declarations(self, prog):\\n+        for md in prog.module_decls:\\n+            if md.is_inline or md.body is not None:\\n+                self.report(md.line, \\\"inline-module\\\",\\n+                            \\\"no inline `module %s { }`; Stage 0 never drops a value of a \\\"\\n+                            \\\"type declared in one, so use a file module\\\" % md.name)\\n+        for td in prog.type_definitions:\\n+            self.report(td.line, \\\"type-alias\\\",\\n+                        \\\"no `type` aliases; use `Int` or a one-field struct\\\")\\n+        for imp in prog.imports:\\n+            self.check_import(imp)\\n+        for decl in prog.structs + prog.enums + prog.traits:\\n+            if decl.name in self.std.reserved_types:\\n+                self.report(decl.line, \\\"prelude-type-name\\\",\\n+                            \\\"`%s` is the name of a std type\\\" % decl.name)\\n+        for s in prog.structs:\\n+            if not s.fields:\\n+                self.report(s.line, \\\"empty-struct\\\", \\\"give every struct at least one field\\\")\\n+        for blk in prog.extern_blocks:\\n+            for fn in blk.functions:\\n+                if fn.is_blocking:\\n+                    self.report(fn.line or blk.line, prog.extensions:\\n+            if not ext.type_params and self.is_generic_type(ext.struct_name):\\n+                self.report(ext.line, \\\"generic-extension-params\\\",\\n+                            \\\"`%s` is generic; write its type parameters in the extension \\\"\\n+                            \\\"head\\\" % ext.struct_name)\\n+            if ext.type_params:\\n+                for m in extension; build it memberwise\\\")\\n+                if any(tp.bounds for tp in ext.type_params):\\n+                    self.report(ext.line, \\\"bounded-extension\\\",\\n+                                \\\"no bounds on an extension's type parameters\\\")\\n+            for m in ext.methods:\\n+                self.check_deinit(m)\\n+                BodyChecker(self, m, ext).run()\\n+        for tr in prog.traits:\\n+            for m in tr.methods:\\n+                self.check_deinit(m)\\n+                if m.body is not None:\\n+                    BodyChecker(self, m, tr).run()\\n+        for st in prog.statics:\\n+            if st.initializer is not None:\\n+                BodyChecker(self, None, None).run_expression(st.initializer)\\n+        for sa in prog.static_asserts:\\n+            BodyChecker(self, None, None).run_expression(sa.condition)\\n+\\n+    def check_deinit(self, m):\\n+        \\\"\\\"\\\"A `deinit` in any extension or trait body.            \\\"source declares no `deinit`\\\")\\n+\\n+    def is_generic_type(self, name):\\n+        \\\"\\\"\\\"Is `name` a generic type: the build's when the build declares it,\\n+        std's otherwise?\\\"\\\"\\\"\\n+        if name in self.facts.types:\\n+            return name in self.facts.generic_types\\n+        return name in self.std.generic_types\\n+\\n+    def check_import(self, imp):\\n+        path = imp.path\\n+        if imp.symbols is None or imp.is_glob:\\n+            self.report(imp.line, \\\"selective-imports\\\",\\n+                        \\\"import names selectively: `import %s.{...}`\\\" % \\\".\\\".join(path))\\n+        head = path[0] if path else \\\"\\\"\\n+        if head == \\\"std\\\":\\n+            module = \\\".\\\".join(path[1:])\\n+ module)\\n+        elif head not in PACKAGE_RELATIVE_HEADS and \\\\\\n+                head not in [name for name, _ in STAGE_PACKAGES]:\\n+            self.report(imp.line, \\\"import-allowlist\\\",\\n+                        \\\"`%s` is neither std, a compiler stage, nor package-relative\\\" % head)\\n+\\n+    # -- written types -------------------------------------------------------\\n+\\n+    def check_types(self):\\n+        stack = [(self.src.program, 1)]\\n+        while stack:\\n+            node, line = stack.pop()\\n+            line = getattr(node, \\\"line\\\", 0) or line\\n+            for f in A.structural_fields(node):\\n+                self._types_in(getattr(node, f.name), line, stack)\\n+\\n+    def _types_in(self, value, line, stack):\\n+        if isinstance(value, A.SawType):\\n+            self.check_type(value, line)\\n+        elif isinstance(value, (list, tuple)):\\n+            for v in value:\\n+                self._types_in(v, line, stack)\\n+        elif dataclasses.is_dataclass(value) and not isinstance(value, type):\\n+            stack.append((value, line))\\n+\\n+    def check_type(self, t, fallback):\\n+        if _type_depth(t) > MAX_TYPE_DEPTH:\\n+            self.report(t.written_line or fallback, \\\"written-type-shape\\\",\\n+                        \\\"a == A.TypeKind.STRUCT and part.struct_name == \\\"Optional\\\":\\n+                self.report(line, \\\"written-type-shape\\\", \\\"write `T?`, not `Optional<T>`\\\")\\n+            if k == `Self` inside a tuple type\\\")\\n+            if k == A.TypeKind.EXISTENTIAL:\\n+                self.report(line, \\\"any-type\\\", \\\"no `any`; dispatch over an enum\\\")\\n+            elif k == A.TypeKind.ARRAY:\\n+                self.report(line, \\\"fixed-array\\\", \\\"no fixed-size arrays; use `Vector`\\\")\\n+            elif k == A.TypeKind.POINTER:\\n+ \\\"\\\"\\\"The typed binding SL:hazards S7 recommends for a constant past `Int.max`.\\\"\\\"\\\"\\n+    if isinstance(value, A.IntLiteral) and annotation is not None \\\\\\n+            and annotation.kind in UNSIGNED_64:\\n+        allowed.add(id(value))\\n+\\n+\\n+def _significant_newlines(toks):\\n+    \\\"\\\"\\\"For each token, whether it is a NEWLINE the parser reads: one directly\\n+    inside `(` or `[` is skipped, as the frozen parser skips it.\\\"\\\"\\\"\\n+    out = [False] * len(toks)\\n+    stack = []\\n+    for i, t in enumerate(toks):\\n+        if t.type in (T.LPAREN, T.LBRACKET, T.LT)\\n+    closers = (T.RPAREN, T.RBRACKET, T.GT)\\n+    depth = 0\\n+    while j < len(toks):\\n+        if toks[j].type in openers:\\n+            depth += 1\\n+        elif j\\n+        j += 1\\n+    return j\\n+\\n+\\n+class Binding:\\n+    \\\"\\\"\\\"A name in scope. `kind` is one of: let, var, param, param-ref,\\n+    param-refvar, pattern, pattern-var, for, closure-param, catch. `depth` is\\n+    the closure nesting it was bound at, so a use from deeper is a capture.\\n+    `borrowed` marks a binding that aliases storage it does not own\\n+ static initializer, walked\\n+    with its lexical scopes.\\\"\\\"\\\"\\n+\\n+    def __init__(self, fc, decl, owner):\\n+        self.fc = fc\\n+        self.facts = fc.facts\\n+        self.decl = {}\\n+        for tp in (getattr(owner, \\\"type_params\\\", ()) or []) + \\\\\\n+                (getattr(decl, \\\"type_params\\\", ()) or []):\\n+            self.type_params[tp.name] =      self.fc.report(line, rule, message)\\n+\\n+    # -- scopes --------------------------------------------------------------\\n+\\n+    def bind(self, name, kind, borrowed=False, type_=None):\\n+ entry ---------------------------------------------------------------\\n+\\n+    def run(self):\\n+        d = self.decl\\n+        for p in d.parameters:\\n+            if p.name == \\\"self\\\":\\n+ --------------------------------------------------------\\n+\\n+    def block_ends(self, block):\\n+        if block is None:\\n+            return False\\n+        if block.final_expr is not                       \\\"returning it as a `Result`\\\")\\n+\\n+    def _tails(self, node, out):\\n+        if isinstance(node, A.Block):\\n+            if node.final_expr is not None:\\n+ A method's receiver\\n+        decides whose method it is when its type is traceable; otherwise a name\\n+        std also declares is judged as std's.\\\"\\\"\\\"\\n+        facts = self.facts\\n+        if isinstance(e, A.FunctionCall):\\n+            if e.name in facts.functions or e.name in facts.types or self.is_local(e.name):\\n+                return\\n+            key = e.name\\n+        elif isinstance(e, A.StructInit):\\n+            # The frozen parser reads any `name(label: ...)` as a construction.\\n+            if e.struct_name in facts.types or e.struct_name in facts.functions:\\n+                return\\n+            key = e.struct_name\\n+        elif isinstance(e, A.EnumInit):\\n+            if e.enum_name in facts.types:\\n+                return\\n+            key = \\\"%s.%s\\\" % (e.enum_name, e.variant_name)\\n+        else:\\n+            if e.method_name in RETIRED_STD_METHODS or (\\n+                    e.method_name == \\\"lock\\\"\\n+                    and any(isinstance(a.value, A.ClosureExpr) for a in e.arguments)):\\n+                self.report(e.line, \\\"std-api\\\",\\n+                            \\\"`%s` is retired in the new std\\\" % e.method_name)\\n+                return\\n+            self.check_through_get(e)\\n+            key = self.method_key(e)\\n+            if key is None:\\n+                return\\n+        if key not in STD_API:\\n+            self.report(e.line, \\\"std-api\\\",\\n+                        \\\"`%s` is not on the compiler's std API allowlist\\\" % key)\\n+\\n+    def check_through_get(self, e):\\n+        obj = e.object\\n+        if _is_get_call(obj) or (isinstance(obj, (A.ForceUnwrap, A.BindOptional))\\n+                                 and _is_get_call(obj.expr)):\\n+            if e.method_name in (\\\"is_some\\\", \\\"is_none\\\"):\\n+                self.report(e.line, \\\"std-api\\\",\\n+                            \\\"a presence test through `get`; (self-hosting) Saw compiler,\\n-and a measurement instrument for the rewrite decision (design 116). It is a\\n-faithful port of `sawc/lexer.py`: same token kinds, same token boundaries, same\\n-1-based `line:col` positions, and the same lex-error positions.\\n-\\n-The Python lexer's **observable output** is the correctness reference;\\n-`LANGUAGE_SPEC.md`'s lexical section is authoritative where the two disagree.\\n-\\n-## Layout\\n-\\n-```\\n-selfhost/lexer/\\n-  Saw.toml          # Blade package manifest (name = \\\"sawlex\\\")\\n-  src/lib.saw       # token model (TokenKind + Token + StringSegment) + `lex` + dump format\\n-  src/main.saw      # the `sawlex` CLI\\n-  tests/*.saw       # blade unit tests, one per token family\\n-```\\n-\\n-Build / run directly with sawc:\\n-\\n-```\\n-.venv/bin/python sawc/sawc.py selfhost/lexer/src/main.saw -o .build/sawlex\\n-.build/sawlex <file.saw>\\n-```\\n-\\n-or via Blade (`blade build` / `blade test`) like the `libs/` packages.\\n-\\n-## Canonical token-dump format\\n-\\n-`sawlex <file.saw>` emits one record per token, newline-separated:\\n-\\n-```\\n-KIND<TAB>line:col<TAB>escaped-text[<TAB>suffix]\\n-```\\n-\\n-* `KIND` — the token kind name (see the mapping table below); identical to the\\n-  Python lexer's `TokenType.name`.\\n-* `line:col` — 1-based, at the token's first byte/character.\\n-* `escaped-text` — the token's canonical text (decoded for strings; underscores\\n-  stripped and the base prefix lowercased for numbers), escaped at the **byte**\\n-  level: `\\\\\\\\` for backslash, `\\\\n` `\\\\t` `\\\\r` for those controls, `\\\\0` for NUL, any\\n-  other control byte (`< 0x20` or `0x7F`) as `\\\\xHH`, and every other byte —\\n-  including raw UTF-8 `>= 0x80` — verbatim.\\n-* `suffix` — a 4th column, present **only** for a fixed-width-suffixed integer\\n-  literal: one of `i8`/`i16`/`i32`/`i64`/`u8`/`u16`/`u32`/`u64`. `255u8` dumps as\\n-  `INT<TAB>1:1<TAB>255<TAB>u8`; every other token stops at the escaped text.\\n-\\n-### An `INTERP_STRING` record carries its segments\\n-\\n-An interpolated string's `escaped-text` is the literal's **raw source spelling**\\n-between the quotes (undecoded), and the record then continues with **one field\\n-per typed segment** (design 268), in source order:\\n-\\n-```\\n-INTERP_STRING<TAB>line:col<TAB>raw<TAB>T:text<TAB>E:line:col:raw-expr...\\n-```\\n-\\n-* `T:` — a literal run, **decoded** (so an escaped brace is a plain `{`/`}`).\\n-* `E:line:col:` — an interpolation, carrying the **raw** text between its braces\\n-  and the exact 1-based source position of its opening `{`.\\n-\\n-An empty `T:` run is never emitted, so two adjacent interpolations produce two\\n-consecutive `E:` fields. No token carries both a suffix and segments, so the\\n-tail is unambiguous. The segments are what a parser consumes: nothing is encoded\\n-into the token's bytes, which is what design 268 replaced (an escaped brace used\\n-to be the two bytes `0x01` + brace — indistinguishable from that same pair\\n-written as content, which silently lost it; SL-238).\\n-\\n-On a lex error the CLI emits a single record and exits nonzero:\\n-\\n-```\\n-ERROR<TAB>line:col<TAB>message\\n-```\\n-\\n-Error **positions and kinds** match the Python lexer; message **prose does not**\\n-(the differential harness compares the `ERROR` tag + position only).\\n-\\n-`tools/dump_tokens.py` emits the byte-identical format from the Python lexer;\\n-`tools/lexdiff.py` (`make lexdiff`) diffs the two dumps over every tracked `.saw`\\n-file. Only a comment that starts its line is a doc comment; `////`\\n-(four or more slashes) and a `///` trailing code on the same line are ordinary\\n-comments.\\n-\\n-`tools/dump_tokens.py --docs` emits the same records from the Python lexer, and\\n-`make lexdiff` sweeps both dumps over the corpus (`--mode tokens|docs` picks one).\\n-\\n-## Kind-name mapping (TokenKind case → dump name)\\n-\\n-The Saw `TokenKind` enum is the model.
